Between Negative Stigma (Cultural Deprivation)
page=1 block=18418727 (x=1 y=61) width=274.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimesB wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and Positive Stigma (Learning Disability): The
page=1 block=18418727 (x=1 y=76) width=264.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimesB wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Historical Development of Two Special Education

Tracks

Ofer Katchergin

page=1 block=11471166 (x=1 y=190) width=175.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Abstract This article posits an updated, broader perspective on the concept of
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=222) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
learning disabilities (LDs) than that accepted in the local Israeli literature, revealing
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=234) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
how it is immersed in class, ethnicity, and culture. This is shown through historical
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=246) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
description, accreditation, and contrasting of the two special education discourses:
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=258) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the ‘‘cultural deprivation’’ discourse and the ‘‘LDs’’ discourse. There are three
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=270) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
sections. Part One presents the theoretical, conceptual, and methodological back-
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=282) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ground of the sociological and discursive debate about LDs. The social-
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=294) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
constructivist model used in an analysis of the two categories is proposed as an
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=306) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
alternative to the clinical–medical model. The de?nitions of LDs and cultural
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=318) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
deprivation accepted in the Israeli discourse are presented in Part Two. The
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=330) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
metamorphoses in the discourse about the category of LDs are uncovered through
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=342) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
reference to their conceptual and historical antecedents. This part discusses the
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=354) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
various understandings and constructions of learning dif?culties. Part Three
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=366) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
examines the textual representation of parents of children with disabilities in both
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=378) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cases, exploring the meanings of guilt, responsibility, and agency in each discourse.
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=390) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The conclusion clari?es the social and political signi?cance of the distinct textual
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=402) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and rhetorical representations. It becomes evident that the discourse on LDs and the
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=414) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
discourse on cultural deprivation are two special education tracks directed at dif-
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=426) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ferent target audiences: the culturally enriched audience, well-off and educated on
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=438) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the one hand, and the Mizrahi audience of limited means and education on the other
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=449) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
hand.
page=1 block=22139800 (x=1 y=461) width=22.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16

Part One
page=2 block=2621810 (x=1 y=74) width=40.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimesB wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Introduction
page=2 block=3277290 (x=1 y=98) width=50.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
Duringthesixth decade ofthe twentiethcentury, twoclinical andeducationalcategories
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
regarding learning and social dif?culties were formed in Israel. One category—that of
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
cultural deprivation—has disappeared from the contemporary Israeli public and
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
academic discourse. In contrast, the second category—that of learning disabilities
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(LDs)—has gathered ever greater and greater momentum and public exposure. The
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
prestige of the ?rst category has been waning due to the long series of social and
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=182) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
professional criticisms that it was subjected to for many years. The prestige of the
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=194) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
second category has been rising though and it is now enjoying an almost unanimous
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=206) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
acceptance among academic researchers as well as educators and practitioners.
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=217) width=311.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Both of those two historical categories purportedly delineated speci?c medical-
page=2 block=22138702 (x=12 y=229) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ized conditions that were very different from regular learning dif?culties: cultural
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
deprivation expressed learning dif?culties and failure emanating from socio-
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
economic and cultural sources, while LDs expressed learning dif?culties and failure
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
emanating from biological sources. Interestingly, to date, no academic efforts have
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
been made in Israeli professional and educational literature to juxtapose those two
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
clinical categories and scrutinize them reciprocally.
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=301) width=211.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
This juxtaposition seems necessary on the backdrop of the historical parallel
page=2 block=22138702 (x=12 y=313) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
evolution of the two special education discourses that made the existence of the two
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
clinical categories possible. Over the years, the two discourses developed in parallel
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
asymptotically establishing distinct territories of work and knowledge. Different
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
disciplines, professionals, and practices developed in order to aid and treat the
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
varying needs of the two types of population that were constituted as subjects of
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
these two special education discourses. Points of contact between the two discourses
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
were almost entirely absent. It is worth mentioning that even the critical sociological
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and educational literature about cultural deprivation ignored its historical and
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
educational twin and the social implications of that simultaneous evolution.
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=421) width=310.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The current article seeks to throw light on the aforementioned silence by
page=2 block=22138702 (x=12 y=433) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
juxtaposing the two special education discourses while emphasizing their central
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=445) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
points of similarity and of divergence. The article delineates the two clinical twin
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=457) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
representations that were constructed in both of the discourses while accentuating
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=469) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
their dissimilarities. Among those, one can ?nd divergent representations of the
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
‘‘self’’ (both of the disabled and of their parents) that were constructed in the
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
framework of the two discourses, different expectations of the two populations and
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=504) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
their families, different causal attributions of the two syndromes and different
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=516) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
educational ideologies in which the two discourses were embedded. The current
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=528) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
article also aims to clarify the importance of the two discourses’ parallel analysis by
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=540) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
interpreting its ?ndings in the framework of Israeli society’s hierarchical ethnic and
page=2 block=22138702 (x=1 y=552) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

class characters (a social aspect that has been dominant since the country
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=26) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
establishment1).
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=35) width=65.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
The claim is made that an analytical juxtaposition of the two special education
page=3 block=22096006 (x=12 y=50) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
discourseshelps torevealthe different assumptions which the educational experts held
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=62) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
with regard to the two populations. It is claimed that, from their very beginning in the
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=74) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
1960s, the two discourses were directed at distinct social populations. Whereas the
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
objects of the cultural deprivation discourse were the Mizrahim, lower class and less
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
educated, the objects of the LD discourse were Ashkenazim, the established and
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=110) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
educated.2
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=119) width=42.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
There are three sections. Part One presents the theoretical and the conceptual
page=3 block=22096006 (x=12 y=134) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
background of the discussion. It presents claims based theoretically on the
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
intellectual ?elds of critical discourse analysis, narratology and sociology of
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
knowledge. It also clari?es its methodological guidelines while delineating the
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
textual corpus that serves as its data.
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=181) width=151.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Part Two presents the de?nitions of LDs and of cultural deprivation accepted in
page=3 block=22096006 (x=12 y=193) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the Israeli discourse as well as their historical transformations. The metamorphoses
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in the discourse in relation to the category of LDs are uncovered through reference
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to their conceptual and historical antecedents. This part reveals the two aforemen-
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tioned discourses’ divergent conceptions and constructions of learning dif?culties:
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Negative stigmatization in the cultural deprivation case vis-a?-vis Positive stigma-
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tization in the case of LDs.
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=265) width=112.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Part Three further exposes the differencesbetween the two discourses by examining
page=3 block=22096006 (x=12 y=277) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the textual representation of parents of children with disabilities and families in both
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cases, as well as the different expert expectations in the two cases. The distinct
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
discursive positioning of the families in both cases (parental inclusionand participation
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
in the LDs case vis-a?-vis parental exclusion in the cultural deprivation case) uncovers
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
important distinctions in the families’ textual representations while exploring the
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
meanings of guilt, responsibility, and agency in each discourse. The learning disabled
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
parent’s constructed pro?le is that of a good parent in potentiality: an imagined ideal
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
parent helping his child to overcome the disability. The culturally deprived parent’s
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
constructed pro?le is that of a pathological case comprising a central component of the
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
child’s problem. The expert literature consistently blurs the learning disabled parent’s
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ethnic and class characteristics while it contrarily highlights the culturally deprived
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
parent’s ethnic and class characteristics. Finally, the article explicates the social and
page=3 block=22096006 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
1 Israel Jewish population was divided from the onset of the state into two distinct ethnic groups:
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=446) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Ashkenazi Jews and Mizrahi Jews. During the 1940s, 1950s, 1960s, and most of the 1970s, the political,
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=458) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
economic, and cultural establishments in Israel were governed by Ashkenazi elites. The Mizrahi
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=467) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
immigrant population suffered from practices of marginalization and exclusion within the Jewish
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=477) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
population in Israel (even though the two populations were about the same size). Moreover, since 1969,
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=486) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the marginalized Mizrahi population outnumbered the Ashkenazi population. For relevant statistical and
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=496) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
demographic data of the aforementioned decades, see Peres (1976). For concurrent data and analysis of
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=505) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ethnic relations in Israel, see Shitrit (2004).
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=515) width=142.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
2 The term Mizrahi (pl. Mizrahim) is a common appellation in Israel for Jews of Middle-Eastern and
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=525) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
North African (mostly Arab) origin. Ashkenazi (pl. Ashkenazim) commonly refers to Jews of Western
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=537) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(mostly European) origin. These terms are often used by Israelis in order to draw cultural, hierarchical
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=546) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
distinctions between the two groups. Traditionally, the term Ashkenazim designated a better symbolic
page=3 block=22140490 (x=1 y=556) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
status, while the term Mizrahim had negative cultural connotations.

Methodology and Corpus Selection
page=4 block=9438149 (x=1 y=193) width=144.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The professional discourse is analyzed by means of discourse analysis and narrative
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
analysis techniques. Using these, the article focuses on the different ways that
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
enable the LD experts to enforce their version of social reality with the aid of
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
various linguistic manipulations. In this article, discourse refers to routine mundane
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
practices that produce, stabilize, and enforce social relations characterized by social
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
control and hierarchical distinction between social groups (Thompson 1984).
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
Narrative refers to routine interpretative devices through which people represent
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
‘‘reality,’’ ‘‘self,’’ and ‘‘disease’’ conceptions to themselves and to others (Riessman
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
2008). Furthermore, these processes are also constructed in written texts through the
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
use of linguistic strategies of symbolic construction (Thompson 1984).
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=325) width=290.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The daily social use of language produces and reproduces various meanings through
page=4 block=22138580 (x=12 y=337) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
its narrative components (Colyar and Holley 2010; Horsdal 2012; Riessman 2008;
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Toolan2001). Claims that aremaderegarding thesocialrealityareoftenperceived asself
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
evident. This is an expressionofthe power of language toproducesuch self evidentiality
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(Fairclough 1992). For example, this is the position of the clinical explanations
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
embedded in professional written texts serving as data in the current article.3
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=394) width=298.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The current research is based on a rich textual corpus which includes two types of
page=4 block=22138580 (x=12 y=409) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
professional and academic literature: Israeli literature about cultural deprivation
page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=432) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=4 block=22138580 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
regarded as central (canonical) in their ?elds as well as other texts.4 The earliest of

On the Social Construction of LDs and Cultural Deprivation
page=5 block=16254313 (x=1 y=277) width=248.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Various researchers, basing themselves on the social construction paradigm, claim
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that clinical knowledge (medical, psychological, and special-pedagogical) that is
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
usually regarded as objective and neutral is, in fact, socially constructed (Bury
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
1986). In terms of this social model, the objects of study of the various clinical
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
professions are not decontextualized neutral and objective objects. Rather they are
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
objectivizations (Berger and Luckman 1967). That is to say that clinical entities are
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
more similar to socially originated inventions than they are to decontextualized
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
discoveries. Clinical entities are constructed within the framework of a medicalizing
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and psychopathologizing model by manipulating scienti?c ideologies, terminolo-
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
gies, and technologies. Nowadays, this model is producing an alarmingly increasing
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
number of clinical categories characterized by distinct mental and physical qualities
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
(Carter et al. 2012; Conrad 1992, 2006, 2007; Riska 2003).8 On the current state of
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=430) width=337.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the medicalization theory and on the different conceptualizations of medicalization
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
within it, see Clarke et al. (2009, 2010). On the various meanings of the concept of
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=456) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
medicalization and on its evolution, see Nye (2003). In developed countries, this
page=5 block=22137404 (x=1 y=468) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
5 Brain damage was one of the most popular terms that predated the learning disabilities terminology in
page=5 block=22125160 (x=1 y=488) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Israel.
page=5 block=22125160 (x=1 y=500) width=20.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=12
6 Among the most prominent ofthese writers are Rivka Kidron, Shimeon Simpson,Shlomo Sharan, Yael

The de?nition of Brain Damage and LDs—delineating the problem and concep-

page=6 block=22151072 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
came into contact with.
page=6 block=22151072 (x=1 y=504) width=96.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

(Hamdani 2003; Simpson 1985; Razi 2006). In the 1950s, 1960s, and 1970s, the

A review of the books and articles in Hebrew on the subject revealed that, since

page=7 block=22092600 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A learning disability refers to a retardation, disorder or delayed development
page=7 block=247746035 (x=12 y=307) width=315.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in one or more of the processes of speech, language, reading, writing,

page=7 block=247746035 (x=12 y=331) width=315.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

page=7 block=247746035 (x=12 y=343) width=315.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
disturbances. It is not the result of mental retardation, sensory deprivation, or
page=7 block=247746035 (x=12 y=355) width=315.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cultural and instructional factors.

Indeed, in Kirk’s LD de?nition, emotional or behavioral disturbances are also

de?nitions of LD, emotional or behavioral causes were already de?ned as non-
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=74) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
causal factors. Otherwise they were simply erased. No later than the late 1960s,
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Kirk’s de?nition was replaced with new de?nitions. Those newer de?nitions are
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
important for the purposes of the current article mainly in their explicit rejection of
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=110) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
environmental, cultural, or economic factors as possible and relevant causes in the
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
development of LDs. The neurological factor has thus become the only relevant
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
etiological cause.14
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=143) width=78.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The LD discourse was characterized from its very beginning by an empathizing
page=8 block=22098502 (x=12 y=158) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and sympathizing portrayal of the human subjects who were constructed by it.
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Comprehensive descriptions were devoted to the psychological frustration, the
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
dif?culties, and even to the allegedly suffering of those labeled by it as they were
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
portrayed as being in a constant struggle against their constitutional weaknesses as
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
well as against the intolerant society. The learning disabled were also described as
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
having intact motivation and intelligence. As a matter of fact, they were often
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
described in the literature as being endowed with higher than average intelligence,
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
capabilities, and potential. Especially interesting was the customary claim made by
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
experts whereby failure in intelligence tests did not necessarily indicate the lack of
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
intellectual capabilities or of cognitive potential on the part of the learning disabled
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
child. Problematic manifestations of personality traits, familial relationships, or
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
emotional dif?culties, in cases in which they were found, were identi?ed as
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
consequences of the organic de?ciency (and not as its generators). For example, in
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the framework of the LD discourse, LDs were a causal factor of problematic family
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
relationships. The opposite process, in which problematic family relationships could
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
have possibly been a causal factor of LDs, was rejected.
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=349) width=232.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
These characteristics of the LD discourse had a positive stigmatic in?uence.
page=8 block=22098502 (x=12 y=361) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Therefore, it is not surprising that this discourse had a strong seductive potential
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
from its inception. The problematic children who were constructed within the
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
framework of the LD discourse did not resemble the previous multitude of brain
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
damaged, brain handicapped, and brain defectives (the concepts that populated the
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
expert literature prior to the introduction of the LD discourse). Likewise, the LDs
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
children did not resemble the parallel discourse of the cultural deprivation children
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
who were characterized as suffering from low intelligence, low motivation, lack of
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
emotional maturity, and criminal-like lines of behavior.
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=456) width=228.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Contrary to the negative stigmatization of the culturally deprived children, not
page=8 block=22098502 (x=12 y=468) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
only was the LD children’s self not contaminated, but also, more often than not,
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
these children’s self came to be marked by augmentative quali?ers.15 In this
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=489) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
manner, those marked as LD have came to be perceived more and more as
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=504) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
intelligent and as motivational. As a consequence, previous medical and
page=8 block=22098502 (x=1 y=516) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=10
14 Extensive reference to the developments and changes in the historical de?nitions of learning
page=8 block=22100780 (x=1 y=541) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
disabilities can be found in Katchergin (2009).

687
page=9 block=256376836 (x=326 y=1) width=12.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=12
123
page=9 block=764218261 (x=299 y=593) width=39.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvSPRING-R wordint.min=1 wordint.max=18
pedagogical discourses (including the parallel cultural deprivation discourse) were
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=26) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
turned upside down: Academic dif?culties that were previously identi?ed with
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=38) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
mental retardation, with intellectual mediocrity, with laziness, or with moral
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=50) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
weakness, have begun to be perceived as indicative of exactly the opposite traits!
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=62) width=335.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The new LDs’ discourse had many advantages. The self of the children who had
page=9 block=22091664 (x=12 y=74) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
academic dif?culties was no longer contaminated, since that dif?culty was
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
delimited to the educational domain alone within its framework. The new discourse
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
enabled the prevention of the neurological susceptibility to cross-over into other
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=110) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
dimensions of the self-concept (such as personality, cognition, psyche, etc.). Thus,
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the children under discussion could become marked as having a coherent,
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
integrative, and healthy self. Their cognitive, mental, and psychological identity
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
remained without a blot on it, their identi?cation as disabled (biologically
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
originated) notwithstanding. Moreover, the neurological susceptibility discussed
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
was conceptualized in an a-deterministic way, a conceptualization that facilitated
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the reception of the new discourse among groups of worried parents. That is, even
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
though the susceptibility was identi?ed as permanent by the expert diagnosticians,
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
they still considered it reparable and reversible for their part. To a large extent, the
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
LDs label had a unique role—it was a kind of positive clinical stigma16,17 that was
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=226) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in stark contrast to alternative clinical pathologizing categories that largely had (and
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
some of them still have) a role of negative stigmatic labels.
page=9 block=22091664 (x=1 y=253) width=245.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The De?nition of Cultural Deprivation—Delineating the Problem and
page=9 block=18748005 (x=1 y=277) width=286.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Conceptualizing it in Environmental Terms
page=9 block=18748005 (x=1 y=289) width=177.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In the past, the term, cultural deprivation (as well as its equivalents, reading
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
deprived and fostering required) was used to de?ne students who failed in their
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
studies for reasons that were identi?ed as socio-economic and cultural. These
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
students were perceived as requiring educational fostering; the term cultural
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
deprivation was coined in the United States after the Second World War and gave
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
expression to a perception that emphasized society’s responsibility to advance
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
neglected groups and to integrate them into the education system, society, and
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
culture (Arieli et al. 1997). The reasons for cultural deprivation were attributed to
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
education methods in the family, which were not appropriate for the Western middle
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
class, its mindset, its language, and the school culture in these societies. A
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
translation of these perceptions into the Israeli context was given major expression
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in the works of researchers and educators such as Frankenstein (1970), who claimed
page=9 block=22128200 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
16 The traditional literature on stigma focuses on identifying factors contributing to the harmful impact of
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=465) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
stigmas on the lives of stigmatized individuals and social groups. In the current article, the term positive
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=477) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
stigma is suggested for social processes in which medicalized labels do not devalue or discredit those
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=486) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
labeled by them and do not spoil their normal identity. On the contrary, in this special case, the
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=496) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
medicalized labels can rather empower the stigmatized.
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=505) width=181.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
17 It is interesting to note that a literal English translation of the Israeli term for culturally deprived
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=515) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(teunei tipuach) is ‘‘in need of nurturing.’’ This means that in Hebrew the term blurs the contextual
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=527) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
elements (cultural and social) linked to it even more than the English term. Nonetheless, in this article, I
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=537) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
chose to use the term culturally deprived which is more widespread in English. As for the Israeli
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=546) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
educational notion of nurture (tipuach) and its local version of culturally deprived (teunei tipuach), see
page=9 block=22133992 (x=1 y=556) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=10 block=5245845 (x=2 y=593) width=40.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvSPRING-R wordint.min=1 wordint.max=18

page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=26) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=38) width=317.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In the early 1960s, the Israeli education system started to grapple with the
page=10 block=22098502 (x=12 y=50) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
problem of the culturally deprived by providing additional funding, like teaching
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=62) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
hours, small classes, teaching aids and special programs, and teaching methods for
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=74) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
schools that were de?ned as culturally deprived. It is interesting to note that,
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
concurrently, similar steps were taken with regard to children identi?ed as having
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
brain damage or were ‘‘organic’’ (later termed learning disabled).
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=110) width=268.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Indeed, in the 1990s, the use of the term culturally deprived in Israel was
page=10 block=22098502 (x=12 y=122) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
abandoned completely (Arieli et al. 1997). ‘‘It was found that it labels population
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
groups on the basis of their ethnic origin, reinforces stigmas, and reduces their
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
chances of extricating themselves from the situation.’’ The use of this term is no
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
longer fashionable; however, it is apparent that the use of its historic twin—LDs—
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
has become more and more widespread. The term LDs has established itself
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
conceptually and practically in educational institutions and places which were
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
previously occupied by other logical discourses.18
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=203) width=204.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The cultural deprivation discourse was characterized by dis-empathizing and
page=10 block=22098502 (x=12 y=217) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
stigmatic portrayal of the human subjects who were constructed by it. The
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
problematic children who were constructed within its framework were conceived as
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
intellectually, emotionally, and morally damaged. Contrary to the case of the
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
learning disabled children, the self-identity of the culturally deprived was
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
cognitively, mentally, and psychically contaminated. Moreover, as opposed to the
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
learning disabled children’s a-deterministic portrayal, the physical susceptibility of
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
culturally deprived children was portrayed mostly in deterministic terms. The origin
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of the susceptibility was indeed conceived as environmental in nature, but still its
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
physical manifestations were regarded as completely irreparable. To a large extent,
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the cultural deprivation label was used as a negative stigmatization (similar to many
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
other clinical categories mentioned in the medicalization literature).
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=349) width=278.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
These two distinct channels of educational discourse and policy (that of cultural
page=10 block=22098502 (x=12 y=361) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
deprivation and that of LDs) developed concurrently in Israel. Therefore, it is
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
surprising that the two special education discourses developed almost without any
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
explicit mutual references. This is especially surprising when one takes into account
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that both focused on young and so-called problematic populations of the education
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
system at the time. Even more notable is this mutual disregard given their common
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
interest in identifying academic–educational problems, in locating their origins, and
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in developing pedagogical, didactic, diagnostic, and therapeutic intervention
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
methods for the purpose of coping with them.
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=456) width=189.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The parallel progress of the two discourses was also expressed in the creation
page=10 block=22098502 (x=12 y=468) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
of an apparent division of labor between experts in education and psychology who
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
focused their attention on the culturally deprived and those who concentrated on
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the population of the brain damaged (at a later stage called the learning disabled).
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=504) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Both sets drew upon psychological and educational models and theories; both
page=10 block=22098502 (x=1 y=516) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
18 It is possible that the growing popularity of the learning disabilities discourse played a role in the

Juxtaposing the Two Syndromes
page=11 block=8716872 (x=1 y=146) width=133.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

achievements, low learning ability, and lack of motivation for learning.’’ To these,
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
he added that ‘‘in the accepted of?cial de?nition in the United States, these
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
components were supplemented by low intelligence quotient, and belonging to a
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
low socio-economic stratum.’’19 As early as 1966, Robinson determined that
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=262) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
‘‘reading deprivation’’ (a term that served in combination with cultural deprivation)

page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
also declared that an objective measure of this was the parameter of father’s
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
occupation (Darin 1984).
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=313) width=103.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Sachs (1971) identi?ed cultural deprivation with ‘‘environmentally instigated
page=11 block=22135102 (x=12 y=325) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
retardation’’ which he termed ‘‘pseudo-retardation’’ and linked the retardation to
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
‘‘cultural inadequacy, poverty, and primitiveness.’’ Daniel (1971) expanded on this
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and explained that ‘‘all the [culturally deprived] children, other than a few
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
exceptions, belong to a low cultural stratum; their parents came from weakened
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Levantine countries, and, even there, belonged to a weakened socio-cultural
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
stratum.’’ He, too, comes to the conclusion that the I.Q. of the culturally deprived is
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
low ‘‘because of a cumulative de?cit.’’
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=409) width=159.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
And so it came about that the subjects of the conceptualization of cultural
page=11 block=22135102 (x=12 y=421) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
deprivation were identi?ed and established in the framework of this discourse as
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
unquali?ed cognitively–intellectually and motivationally–morally at one and the

same time. A distorted mirror image of the organically damaged (the predecessor of
page=11 block=22135102 (x=1 y=456) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
19 Darin wasamong the pioneers of learning disabilities research in Israel. In his writings, he gave only a
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=478) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
marginal place to the subject of cultural deprivation. These remarks of his regarding cultural deprivation
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=489) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
are taken from a subchapter devoted to ‘‘the development and education of children who are cognitively
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=499) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
retarded.’’ A subparagraph in this subchapter is entitled ‘‘the culturally deprived child.’’ This almost
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=508) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
anecdotal reference, the laconic de?nition, the identi?cation of cultural deprivation with a ‘‘low mental
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=518) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ability’’ (both in its formal de?nition and equallyby placing the discussion about cultural deprivation in a
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=527) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=537) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
about learning disabilities in the framework of which he emphasized, among other things, the ‘‘high
page=11 block=22129130 (x=1 y=546) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mental ability’’ of the ‘‘learning disabled,’’ their abundance of motivation, and the great importance of

page=12 block=22086698 (x=1 y=62) width=238.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Etiological Differences
page=12 block=6160814 (x=1 y=86) width=94.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In contrast to the endogenous delineation of LDs, from the outset, the phenomenon
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=110) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of cultural deprivation was delineated as exogenous. For example, Stahl (1977)
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
stated that ‘‘The term ‘culturally deprived students’ refers to those children with
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
dif?culty in their school studies for environmental reasons.’’ Various environmental
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
models were developed which identi?ed the causes of cultural deprivation with
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
cultural, socio-economic, or family factors.21 The various models were trying to
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=167) width=337.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
create a link between the problematic environmental conditions in which the
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
children were raised and the personality and behavioral qualities which developed in
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
them as a result of these conditions. These conditions were considered to be an
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
expression of backwardness both in preserving this backwardness and as its
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
outcome, and as ones that justi?ed the need for educational projects for intervention
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and fostering.
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=241) width=56.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The various written works present an index of characteristics attributed to the
page=12 block=22126882 (x=12 y=253) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
culturally deprived characteristics that were perceived as deriving directly from the
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
backward lifestyle in which they were formed. Thus, it is possible to outline the
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
pro?le of the culturally deprived self—that self that was shaped by the expert
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
discourse on it. A central characteristic attributed to the culturally deprived was
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
their lack of motivation to learn and to progress.22
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=310) width=207.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The experts made extensive use of concepts like lack of motivation, absence of
page=12 block=22126882 (x=12 y=325) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mobility, weak desire for achievement, dependence on external authority, and lack
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of internal driving force to characterize the main aspects of cultural deprivation and
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
also to provide an explanation for the long-term survival of the weaknesses of these
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
students in the various educational frameworks. The weakness of the Mizrahi
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
culture and the weakness of character of the culturally deprived student were
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
melded together in the professional literature:
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=397) width=187.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
‘‘Many anthropological studies have shown that cultural style plays a decisive
page=12 block=22126882 (x=12 y=409) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
part in the creation of the motivational structure of personality in earliest
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
childhood’’ declared Emerant (1968), accentuating that ‘‘learning disruptions in
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
the ?eld of motivation are common on the backdrop of the crisis of moving from
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
one culture to another.’’ ‘‘Our assumption is that this student will display little
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=456) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
motivation to study, because those causes of motivation that are linked to studying
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=468) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
are activated in his personality only in a mediocre or weak manner,’’ emphasized
page=12 block=22126882 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=110) width=60.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Another important characteristic attributed to the culturally deprived is the so-
page=13 block=22097566 (x=12 y=122) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
called thinking de?ciencies from which they were considered to be suffering,
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
culturally deprived in the various educational frameworks. Terms such as damaged
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8

page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
his student, who represented the spirit of the way in which cultural deprivation was
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
treated in the professional educational literature.
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=253) width=198.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Cognitive images of damaged intelligence were bound up with cultural images of
page=13 block=22097566 (x=12 y=265) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
backwardness and Mizrahi ‘‘eastern irrationality.’’ In the opinion of expert
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
educators, it was only natural that the so-called irrational Levantine culture would
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
give rise, in the ?eld of education, to a damaged cognitive structure characteristic of
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
irrational thinking whose family sources were based on a backward culture. Cultural
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
deprivation and its educational cognates combined with other social domains in
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
which the experts made an effort to create a hierarchical distinction between
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
problematic cultural expressions of the backward Levant and the standard social
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
patterns of the modern West.24 Moreover, cognitive imagery about intellectual
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=358) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
weakness was also integrated into professional perceptions and positions about
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
poverty and socio-economic distress that were identi?ed with the creators of
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
thinking deprivation.
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=397) width=85.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
To top this off, additional major characteristics attributed to culturally deprived
page=13 block=22097566 (x=12 y=409) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
students related to the emotional and behavioral aspects of their personality. These
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
students were identi?ed as emotionally immature on the one hand and as displaying
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
criminal behavior on the other hand.25 The culturally deprived student was said to
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=442) width=337.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
‘‘be unable to withstand temptations and to delay grati?cation of disruptive needs’’
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=456) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and that he has ‘‘a special negative attitude [to studies]’’ (Minkowitz 1969), that he
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=468) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
is egocentric, impulsive, and emotional (Stahl 1977), that he expresses no interest in
page=13 block=22097566 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
23 See, for example, Ortar (1966), Eiger (1975); Minkowitz (1969), Frankenstein (1970, 1981), Shalgi

page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=98) width=303.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In stark opposition to this, in the LD discourse, there is a complete disregard of
page=14 block=22095070 (x=12 y=110) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the cultural and socio-economic contexts as a relevant component in the
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
conceptualization or the construction of the disabilities.
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=134) width=227.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
So, under the aegis of the burgeoning discourse on cultural deprivation, its expert
page=14 block=22095070 (x=12 y=146) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
proponents developed a kind of index of negative characteristics which related to
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the various aspects of the culturally deprived student’s personality. With regard to
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
each aspect, ranges of personality weaknesses and defects were identi?ed which the
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
culturally deprived allegedly suffered from. The various qualities which composed
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the imaginary pro?le of the culturally deprived together delineated a weak self with
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
defective functioning (Ben-Eliyahu 1976). The defects which characterized these
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
qualities were together identi?ed as symptoms of a defective self as noted by
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Frankenstein (1969).26
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=238) width=92.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In this way, the culturally deprived discourse produced a syndrome whose
page=14 block=22095070 (x=12 y=253) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
environmental (cultural and socio-economic) roots are expressed in the personality
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
structure of those af?icted with it.27
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=274) width=147.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Summarizing this part, the current article seeks to point out the fact that in
page=14 block=22095070 (x=12 y=289) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
parallel to the drafting of the aforementioned cultural deprivation pro?le, there was
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
a simultaneous drafting of a clinical pro?le that eventually came to be known as
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
learning disabled. The representations of the ‘‘self’’ of both of these special
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
education subjects were produced as distinct and different. Whereas the culturally
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
deprived pro?le revolved around stigmatic characteristics interpreted connotatively
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as negative along with a disempathic description of its subjects, the learning
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
disabled pro?le revolved, from the beginning, around largely similar characteristics
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
interpreted this time connotatively as positive stigma.28
page=14 block=22095070 (x=1 y=382) width=227.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
26 See also Minkowitz (1969) regarding this defective structure.
