Part Three: Between Parents and Experts
page=15 block=11993213 (x=1 y=25) width=183.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimesB wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The Importance of Sensitivity to Parents
page=15 block=10879176 (x=1 y=50) width=166.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
A repeated motif in the LDs discourse deals with the great sensitivity that must be
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=74) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
displayed in dealings with the parents of childrenwith dif?culties. Thevarious scholars
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
emphasized over and over the empathy they had for the parents’ concerns. They also
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
repeatedly instructed future practitioners to be sensitive to these worries. The explicit
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=109) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
statements about the importance of sensitivity were supported by a series of textual
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=121) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
mechanisms whose intertwiningcreated aconventional rhetoricof empathization with
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=133) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
parents, their children, and the suffering of both. Rhetorical mechanisms of
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=145) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
empathization were directed at minimizing negative connotations, whichwere usually
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=157) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
identi?ed with disabilities in the structure and function of the brain.29
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=166) width=275.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Another central element which gave expression to the sensitivity and the
page=15 block=22108054 (x=12 y=181) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
identi?cation of the experts with the parents of children with dif?culties can be
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
noted in the demoralization of learning problems and dif?culties in the discourse on
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
LDs. That is to say that in the framework of the new discourse, work was done on
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the negation and intensive conversion of moral and normative idioms that at times
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
were identi?ed as factors relevant to the understanding of learning dif?culties in
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
children. Over and over again, writers emphasized that children with dif?culties
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
should not be related to as having tendencies of lack of motivation, lack of curiosity
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
or laziness. Demoralization was also given expression in relation to the role of
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
parents and the family. They were no longer to be considered guilty or responsible
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for causing their child’s dif?culties.30
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=298) width=154.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In contrast to pedagogical-moralistic theories, the ?rst experts on disabilities in
page=15 block=22108054 (x=12 y=313) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Israel claimed that the parents should be helped to rid themselves of possible guilt
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
feelings by uncovering the ‘‘real’’ reasons (that is to say, the biological ones) for the
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
disabilities. The development of the disability discourse was characterized by the
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
disappearance of moral indicators for disabilities; a disappearance that may have
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
eased the espousal of the discourse for many groups of parents.31
page=15 block=22108054 (x=1 y=370) width=269.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The Image of the Good Parent: The Adequate and Helper Parent
page=15 block=17434556 (x=1 y=396) width=266.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The LDs literature addressed the parents as if they were ‘‘every’’ parent, stripping
page=15 block=22092772 (x=1 y=420) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
them of any particular markings of social and group af?liations. Nonetheless, the
page=15 block=22092772 (x=1 y=432) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
29 A detailed description of the various empathization mechanisms intended to minimize the negative
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=453) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
connotations that were identi?ed with dysfunctions of the brain structure and functions in the learning
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=465) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
disabilities discourse is forthcoming in an article of mine.
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=474) width=189.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
30 As opposed to psychological and psychiatric moral theories that characteristically blamed parental
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=484) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
dysfunction as a central factor in the formation of disabilities. Theories like this include Bettelheim’s
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=496) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
‘‘cold mother’’ theory (1967) and Ling’s schizophrenogenic family (see Hartwell 1996; Singh 2004).
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=505) width=329.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
31 This does not mean that the discourse of disabilities lacked moral aspects, but that these began to take
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=515) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
on a different quality. An expression of this can be found in the model of the ideal parent and in the
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=527) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
creation of a system of norms obligating the parent of a learning disabled child. For example, the good
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=537) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
parent was identi?ed as non-judgmental, inclusive, helping his children with their work, involved in the
page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=546) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=15 block=22138180 (x=1 y=556) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
disabilities discourse, and like a student in keeping up-to-date with expert knowledge.

page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=26) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
hinted at the real parent audience which has been identi?ed as relevant to the
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=38) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
disability discourse: ‘‘The parents are required to devote energy and time to provide
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=50) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
many motivational experiences to their children. Due to defective concentration and
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=62) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
listening, the child will not persevere in these activities alone, and he needs an adult

page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the child to the park, to the beach, on trips, and thereby give him the required trust,’’
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
stated Sharan and Sharan (1969), and other experts reiterated the need to devote
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=110) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
quality time to the children and how this contributed to their development. The
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
diverse recommendations hint at the parent population likely to be characterized by
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
certain entertainment and enrichment habits. Furthermore, this audience must be
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
endowed with the resources of free time (as well as economic resources) such that
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the recommendations would be meaningful and implementable for them.
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=170) width=298.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The parents were perceived as those that can and are expected to contribute their
page=16 block=22099126 (x=12 y=181) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
time to the act of teaching itself and to developing the children’s basic skills: ‘‘The
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
parents must read age-appropriate books to their children from a very early age and
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
keep them interested. This activity should be part of everyday life and should be
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
pleasurable for both sides’’ (Darin 1984).32 They should lend a hand and help their
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=226) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
children in preparing them for exams or in guidance and practical assistance (Segal
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
1969). The home environment constitutes a complementary, essential, and integral
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
part of school instruction. Parents, identi?ed as ‘‘an in?uential factor in the progress
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of the student’’, are required to select reading material or, at least, to help with their
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
reading, for the children’s independent work – materials like ‘‘articles from journals,
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
short stories, novels, and imaginative stories.’’
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=301) width=190.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
In order for parents to help their children scholastically in the best possible way,
page=16 block=22099126 (x=12 y=313) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
they were certainly expected to keep up-to-date with professional literature
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
published in the ?eld of disabilities, and in general to insure that they were aware of
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the knowledge building up in the ?eld. Segal (1969) had already stressed the
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
importance of ‘‘reading books, talking with other parents, participating in lectures
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
and obtaining professional guidance’’ as essential channels for parents to ‘‘acquire
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
suf?cient understanding and knowledge to give adequate help to the child.’’ Many
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
authors even add references to recommended literature and appendices containing
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lists of books for the parents to consult.
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=409) width=164.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The parents were required to help their children not only in the educational-
page=16 block=22099126 (x=12 y=421) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
learning sense but also in emotional and social senses. That is to say that they were
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tasked not only with learning and teaching in the academic sense but also with
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
studying the emotional, mental, behavioral, and social implications of their
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=456) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
children’s dif?culties, so that, as parents, it would be easier for them to understand,
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=468) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
to include, and to accept them. The many expectations and enormous hopes that the
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
experts assigned to the parents were accompanied by demands that they consult with
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
them frequently and that they should develop concepts about proper parenting.
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=504) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Concurrently, normative claims began to surface from the parents, which addressed
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=516) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
their needs and even their obligation to develop skills in these ?elds (academic,
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=528) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
emotional, and social) so as to cope effectively with their learning disabled children.
page=16 block=22099126 (x=1 y=540) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

Parent–Expert Cooperation
page=17 block=7209925 (x=1 y=193) width=110.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The authors’ recommendations did not only relate to the ways in which the parents
page=17 block=22159412 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=17 block=22159412 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

Guilt and Responsibility
page=18 block=6555044 (x=1 y=361) width=100.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Since the parents are perceived as central factors with responsibility for the
page=18 block=22133632 (x=1 y=384) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8

indications of pathology in children with non-normative parental conduct.34 And

as a ?gure of an active social agent that is commanded by means of the rhetoric of
page=18 block=22133632 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
responsibility and parental concern to keep track of the proper development of his
page=18 block=22133632 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
child and to really save him, if necessary.

parenting thus requires the development of habitual tendencies of continual concern

?gures were de?ned by means of particular ethnic, class, and cultural signi?ers,
page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
using disempathic rhetoric. Rather than the ‘‘every parent’’ of the brain damage
page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
discourse, the parents of the culturally deprived were perceived as being able to
page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
ones. Hence, from the ?rst, the discourse on cultural deprivation was characterized
page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
by particularization of the parental image which was its object. It also created a

separation between the social groups they belonged to and groups identi?ed as

normative.
page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=253) width=43.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
Parents of culturally deprived children were described as un?t in many ways
page=20 block=22137490 (x=12 y=265) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
(intellectually, motivationally, in terms of language, emotionally and socially) to

help with advancing their children. The various researchers emphasized the

ality.35 These parents of culturally deprived children were described as having
page=20 block=22137490 (x=1 y=322) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
negative qualities such as pessimism and defeatism.

Summary
page=26 block=2753837 (x=1 y=265) width=42.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimesB wordint.min=1 wordint.max=17
In summary, it seems that under the aegis of the two discourses, both the cultural
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
deprivation and the LDs ones, two special educational tracks developed in Israel.
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The two tracks de?ned differentially the likelihood of their distinct populations to
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
succeed or, conversely, to fail.40 The two tracks formed and organized around
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=322) width=337.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
different representations of the ‘‘self’’ that were attributed to the populations that
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
served as their subjects of study: The cultural deprivation track was organized
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
around a contaminated self (cognitively, morally and behaviorally). The brain-
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
damaged track (eventually the LDs track) was organized around a rehabilitated self
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in these respects.
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=385) width=70.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The logics (raisons d’etre) of the two tracks can be explained in terms of
page=26 block=22145744 (x=12 y=397) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
contrasting educational ideas. The logic of the cultural deprivation track can be
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
characterized as an evolutionary idea of progress (the children are to be advanced as
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
far as possible, although the disabilities are not totally reparable). The logic of the
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
LD track can be characterized as a mechanistic idea of correction (the disabilities or
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
rather the disabilities’ manifestations are reparable).
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=456) width=213.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
That is, the cultural deprivation discourse re?ected and expressed evolutionary
page=26 block=22145744 (x=12 y=468) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
pedagogical trends by which the support to be given to culturally deprived children
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
was of great importance, even though it was to be expected that their level of
page=26 block=22145744 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
40 Procedures of exclusion, segregation, and institutional tracking along ethnic lines of Mizrahi Jewish
page=26 block=22113442 (x=1 y=515) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
children elicited hardly any opposition, as they were taking place under the banner of ‘‘tipuach’’ (the
page=26 block=22113442 (x=1 y=527) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Israeli ‘‘nurture’’ version of the Cultural Deprivation discourse). For example, a considerable number of
page=26 block=22113442 (x=1 y=537) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Mizrahi childrenhave been placed in vocational schools by this procedure (Mizrachi 2004; Swirski 1990;
page=26 block=22113442 (x=1 y=546) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Shavit 1990) and referred to special education programs (Tsur 1991). According to these authors, these
page=26 block=22113442 (x=1 y=556) width=337.000000 height=3.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
processes have played a central role in shaping the current strati?ed social makeup of Israeli society.

development (educationally speaking—their abilities, capacities, and as a result

page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=62) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
self and would not be totally reparable (or reversible).

As opposed to that, the LDs discourse re?ected and expressed corrective
page=27 block=22099750 (x=12 y=86) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8

page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
capacities, these children purportedly had no anomalous cultural, socio-economic, or
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=158) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
familial history. Therefore, an adequate enough mechanistic intervention could
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=170) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
uncover those abilities and capacities (without regard to their degree of severity).
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=181) width=325.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
The progress idea was embedded in a phylogenetic perception of human
page=27 block=22099750 (x=12 y=193) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
development. In the cultural deprivation discourse, it was not possible to separate
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the child’s defective self from the problematic environmental circles which
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
surrounded it. That child’s dif?culties (as well as their origins) were not his alone
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
but were rather part of a broad backward population. The danger immanent in such a
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
problematic child is not manifested only (or mainly) in his future failures but also in
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
the negative in?uences of these failures on society at large. That being the case, the
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
treatment methods had to provide an answer to a collective problem, not just to an
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
individual problem of this or that child. Moreover, since the problem was perceived
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
as collective, it was not an option to ?nd an overall solution to the problem of the
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
individual child. Only a structural and systematic treatment of the backward
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=313) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
populations—a treatment whose aim was to ameliorate them over time—could
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
eventually be manifested in the form of a child having an unblemished self.
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=337) width=312.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The correction notion was embedded in an ontogenetic perception of human
page=27 block=22099750 (x=12 y=349) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
development. The child’s defective self was examined in light of psychological
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
theories and developmental models that focused on the developmental phases of that
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
particular human individual from birth, on dif?culties and deviations from the
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
normal stages, and on adequate treatment methods. That child’s dif?culties (as well
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as their origins) were self-contained, and hence it was possible to locate, isolate and
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
‘‘surgically’’ ?x them. Because of that, the aim of the treatment was ?rst to advance
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the child’s development (in the ontogenetic-particular sense) as an individual, to
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
speed up that development, or to remove the barriers placed by the disabilities
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
before that development.
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=456) width=102.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The phylogenetic understanding of the dif?culties of the culturally deprived was
page=27 block=22099750 (x=12 y=468) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
manifested, therefore, in identifying these dif?culties as qualitatively different from
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
similar (and also from seemingly identical) dif?culties of children who did not
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
belong to the culturally deprived population (i.e., cultured or normal children as
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=504) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
conceived by the experts). So, culturally deprived children were socially constructed
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=516) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
as essentially different from other children.
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=528) width=177.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The ontogenetic understanding of the dif?culties of the learning disabled was
page=27 block=22099750 (x=12 y=540) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
manifested, on the other hand, in identifying these dif?culties as mere statistical
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=552) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
variants on a mainly normative developmental continuum. It is true that at times
page=27 block=22099750 (x=1 y=564) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6

page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=122) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
development conceptualizations that characterized them, re?ected historically the
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=134) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
unequal and hierarchical ethno-class social structure of Israeli society, at the same
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=146) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
time that they were contributing to its social reproduction.
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=158) width=240.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The expectations and recommendations of the experts should be viewed in the
page=28 block=22097254 (x=12 y=170) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
broader educational and social context—during the 1960s, a segregationist
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=181) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
educational policy was engendered in Israel; a policy which has became the
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
prevailing one (Ziv 1980). Since the establishment of the State of Israel in 1948 and
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
up to the 1960s, the educational ideology of equality in education was dominant.
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
The meaning of this was that, theoretically at least, there were equal conditions and
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
equal curricula in all of the schools (Ziv 1980). Many have written about the
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
formation of the segregationist educational ideology during the 1960s focusing
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
mainly on the now discredited discourse of cultural deprivation. That ideology
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
developed against the background of the incoming waves of immigration to Israel
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
from states which were conceived as backward, the negative perceptions of many of
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the immigrants on the part of the indigenous population, and the widespread local
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=301) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
public belief in the validity of the I.Q. test.41
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=310) width=185.000000 height=8.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
The simultaneous existence of the LDs discourse (a discourse that emerged in
page=28 block=22097254 (x=12 y=325) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Israel in those very years) highlights an otherwise hidden aspect in the historical
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
formation of the aforementioned segregationist ideology: The currently rejected
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
widely critiqued cultural deprivation category did not emerge in an educational
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
vacuum devoid of alternative patho-educational conceptualizations. It was the
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=373) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
brain-damaged category (that eventually became the LDs category) which emerged
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=385) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
simultaneously serving as a contrasting category to it. Subjects divided historically
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=397) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
by ethnicity, and class were re?ected and produced in this alternative LDs category.
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=409) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
It seems that this special education track of LDs could have been to a large extent
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=421) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the historical parallel of the cultural deprivation track. That is to say that the LDs
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=433) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
track tended to serve mainly the Ashkenazi, well-established, educated, and well-off
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
population.42 It seems that the LDs track was used as a special education alternative
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=454) width=337.000000 height=7.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
for the strong parents.
page=28 block=22097254 (x=1 y=468) width=90.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
41 These trends are expanded further in Swirsky (1988).
page=28 block=22105810 (x=1 y=531) width=184.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=28 block=22105810 (x=1 y=544) width=337.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4

of disability experts in my forthcoming article.

Conclusion: Differential Expectations
page=29 block=10616957 (x=1 y=25) width=162.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimesB wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Distinct etiological attributions as well as differential interpretations of identical
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=50) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
symptomatology reinforced the experts’ prognostic expectations and practical and
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=62) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
policy recommendations regarding how the children with dif?culties should be dealt
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=74) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
with and also re?ected those expectations and recommendations. The critical
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=86) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
literature is full of descriptions of the low expectations of the educational experts of
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=98) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the culturally deprived children. These low expectations are associated in that
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=109) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
literature with the term ‘‘the Pygmalion effect’’ which negatively contributed to the
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=121) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
defective molding of these children in the image of their widespread negative
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=133) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
imageries. In accordance with this, the destructive policy consequences suited to
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=145) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
these experts’ positions regarding the culturally deprived population were exten-
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=157) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
sively discussed in written texts.
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=169) width=133.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
‘‘The question is raised [remarked Darin as early as 1971- O.K.] by many
page=29 block=22110382 (x=12 y=181) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
specialists in remedial teaching: what is the difference between the remedial
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=193) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
teaching of language, reading, writing and arithmetic dif?culties originating in
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=205) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
abnormalities of the functioning of the brain, and the remedial teaching of
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=217) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
learning dif?culties originating in an environmental background?’’ And, in the
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=229) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
same vein, we can broaden the question and consider whether there were any
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=241) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
differences between the expectations of experts of culturally deprived children and
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=253) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
their expectations of learning disabled children? If indeed, there were such
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=265) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
differences we should ponder how, they manifested themselves. Moreover,
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=277) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
attention should be paid to the differences in the recommendations for methods of
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=289) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
care and assistance.
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=301) width=82.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
In retrospect, it is surprising to realize what a large difference there was between
page=29 block=22110382 (x=12 y=313) width=326.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the expectations of the disabilities experts regarding the children who had
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=325) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9
dif?culties assessed as being based on an organic background and their expectations
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=337) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
regarding the culturally deprived children. More often than not, the dif?culties
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=349) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
described were the same dif?culties, the failures were the same failures, and the
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=361) width=337.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
clinical picture was also identical in both cases. However, while concerning the
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=372) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
culturally deprived children, the disabilities experts adopted the customary and
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=384) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
accepted motifs ‘‘failure expected in advance’’ and ‘‘the need to lower expecta-
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=396) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tions,’’ concerning the learning disabled children they adopted different motifs.
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=408) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Concerning these LD children, they developed a positive stance aided by idioms,
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=420) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
motifs, and argumentations that were embedded in the semantic ?eld of success. It
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=432) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
appears that the boundaries of the individual potential were understood, concep-
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=444) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tualized, and constructed very differently in the two cases.
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=456) width=240.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
While the ?rst special education track served many children as an exclusive route
page=29 block=22110382 (x=12 y=468) width=326.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
that relegated them to the margins of society, the second special educational track
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=480) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
served other children in a different way, seemingly (even only potentially) as a
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=492) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
supportive, inclusive, and integrative route. Whereas the educational and social
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=504) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
manifestations of the ?rst track were extensively examined and studied, the
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=516) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
educational and social manifestations of the second track still wait for research and
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=528) width=337.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
examination.
page=29 block=22110382 (x=1 y=540) width=52.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=AdvPTimes wordint.min=1 wordint.max=16
